<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="113.63px"/><col width="502.13px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:30</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 29</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FIFOEN:</span>FIFO mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FIFO mode is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FIFO mode is enabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">M1</span>: Word length</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit must be used in conjunction with bit 12 (M0) to determine the word length. It is set or cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">M[1:0] = ‘00’: 1 Start bit, 8 Data bits, n Stop bit</p><p class="TableIndent">M[1:0] = ‘01’: 1 Start bit, 9 Data bits, n Stop bit</p><p class="TableIndent">M[1:0] = ‘10’: 1 Start bit, 7 Data bits, n Stop bit</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>In 7-bit data length mode, the smartcard mode, LIN master mode and Auto baud rate (0x7F and 0x55 frames detection) are not supported.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 27:26</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 25:21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DEAT[4:0]</span>: Driver enable assertion time</p><p class="TableIndent">This 5-bit value defines the time between the activation of the DE (Driver Enable) signal and the beginning of the start bit. It is expressed in <span style="font-size : 10.00pt;">lpuart_ker_ck</span> clock cycles. For more details, refer <a href="Universal_synchronous__47_asynchronous_receiver_transmitter_%28USART__47_UART%29.htm#XREF_59470__24_5_16_Hardware_flow_control_and_RS485_Driver_Enable24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 40.5.20: RS232 hardware flow control and RS485 Driver Enable</a>.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="130.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 20:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DEDT[4:0]</span>: Driver enable deassertion time</p><p class="TableIndent">This 5-bit value defines the time between the end of the last stop bit, in a transmitted message, and the de-activation of the DE (Driver Enable) signal.It is expressed in <span style="font-size : 10.00pt;">lpuart_ker_ck</span> clock cycles. For more details, refer <a href="#XREF_98042__27_3_12_RS232_Hardware_flow_control_and_RS485_Driver_Enabl24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 41.4.13: RS232 hardware flow control and RS485 Driver Enable</a>.</p><p class="TableIndent">If the LPUART_TDR register is written during the DEDT time, the new data is transmitted only when the DEDT and DEAT times have both elapsed.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMIE</span>: Character match interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A LPUART interrupt is generated when the CMF bit is set in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MME</span>: Mute mode enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit activates the mute mode function of the LPUART. When set, the LPUART can switch between the active and mute modes, as defined by the WAKE bit. It is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receiver in active mode permanently</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Receiver can switch between mute mode and active mode. </p></td></tr><tr height="70.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">M0</span>: Word length</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used in conjunction with bit 28 (M1) to determine the word length. It is set or cleared by software (refer to bit 28 (M1) description).</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WAKE</span>: Receiver wake-up method</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit determines the LPUART wake-up method from mute mode. It is set or cleared by software.   </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Idle line</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Address mark</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="132.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PCE</span>: Parity control enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit selects the hardware parity control (generation and detection). When the parity control is enabled, the computed parity is inserted at the MSB position (9th bit if M = 1; 8th bit if M = 0) and parity is checked on the received data. This bit is set and cleared by software. Once it is set, PCE is active after the current byte (in reception and in transmission).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Parity control disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Parity control enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PS</span>: Parity selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit selects the odd or even parity when the parity generation/detection is enabled (PCE bit set). It is set and cleared by software. The parity is selected after the current byte.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Even parity</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Odd parity</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PEIE</span>: PE interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: An LPUART interrupt is generated whenever PE = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXEIE</span>: Transmit data register empty</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A LPUART interrupt is generated whenever TXE/TXFNF =1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TCIE</span>: Transmission complete interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: An LPUART interrupt is generated whenever TC = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXNEIE</span>: Receive data register not empty</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A LPUART interrupt is generated whenever ORE = 1 or RXNE/RXFNE = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IDLEIE</span>: IDLE interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: An LPUART interrupt is generated whenever IDLE = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TE</span>: Transmitter enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the transmitter. It is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Transmitter is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Transmitter is enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>During transmission, a low pulse on the TE bit (“0” followed by “1”) sends a preamble (idle line) after the current word. In order to generate an idle character, the TE must not be immediately written to 1. In order to ensure the required duration, the software can poll the TEACK bit in the LPUART_ISR register.</p><p class="RegTNote_">When TE is set there is a 1 bit-time delay before the transmission starts.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RE</span>: Receiver enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the receiver. It is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receiver is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Receiver is enabled and begins searching for a start bit</p></td></tr><tr height="165.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UESM</span>: LPUART enable in Stop mode </p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is cleared, the LPUART is not able to wake up the MCU from low-power mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, the LPUART is able to wake up the MCU from low-power mode, provided that the LPUART clock selection is <span data-keyref="~sci3_hsi">HSI16</span> or LSE in the RCC. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: LPUART not able to wake up the MCU from low-power mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: LPUART able to wake up the MCU from low-power mode. When this function is active, the clock source for the LPUART must be <span data-keyref="~sci3_hsi">HSI16</span> or LSE (see RCC chapter) </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>It is recommended to set the UESM bit just before entering low-power mode and clear it on exit from low-power mode. </p></td></tr><tr height="180.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">UE</span>: LPUART enable</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is cleared, the LPUART prescalers and outputs are stopped immediately, and current operations are discarded. The configuration of the LPUART is kept, but all the status flags, in the LPUART_ISR are reset. This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: LPUART prescaler and outputs disabled, low-power mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1: LPUART enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>To enter low-power mode without generating errors on the line, the TE bit must be reset before and the software must wait for the TC bit in the LPUART_ISR to be set before resetting the UE bit. </p><p class="RegTNote_">The DMA requests are also reset when UE = 0 so the DMA channel must be disabled before resetting the UE bit.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.3 LPUART control register 2 (LPUART\_CR2)

Address offset: 0x04

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">rw</span></p></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"></td></tr></table></div>


<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="80.09px"/><col width="534.50px"/><tr height="269.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADD[7:0]</span>: Address of the LPUART node </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits give the address of the LPUART node in mute mode or a character code to be recognized in low-power or Run mode:</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>In mute mode: they are used in multiprocessor communication to wake up from mute mode with 4-bit/7-bit address mark detection. The MSB of the character sent by the transmitter should be equal to 1. In 4-bit address mark detection, only ADD[3:0] bits are used.</p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>In low-power mode: they are used for wake up from low-power mode on character match.<br/>When WUS[1:0] is programmed to 0b00 (WUF active on address match), the wake-up from low-power mode is performed when the received character corresponds to the character programmed through ADD[6:0] or ADD[3:0] bitfield (depending on ADDM7 bit), and WUF interrupt is enabled by setting WUFIE bit. <span style="font-kerning:none;">The MSB of the character sent by transmitter should be equal to 1.</span></p><p class="RegTBullet2">–<span style="padding-right:16pt ;">​</span>In Run mode with mute mode inactive (for example, end-of-block detection in ModBus protocol): the whole received character (8 bits) is compared to ADD[7:0] value and CMF flag is set on match. An interrupt is generated if the CMIE bit is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits can only be written when the reception is disabled (RE = 0) or when the USART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MSBFIRST</span>: Most significant bit first</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: data is transmitted/received with data bit 0 first, following the start bit. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: data is transmitted/received with the MSB (bit 7/8) first, following the start bit. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DATAINV: </span>Binary data inversion</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Logical data from the data register are send/received in positive/direct logic. (1 = H, 0 = L) </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Logical data from the data register are send/received in negative/inverse logic. (1 = L, 0 = H). The parity bit is also inverted.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXINV: </span>TX pin active level inversion</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: TX pin signal works using the standard logic levels (V<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">DD</span></sub> = 1/idle, Gnd = 0/mark) </p><p class="TableIndent">1: TX pin signal values are inverted (V<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">DD</span></sub> = 0/mark, Gnd = 1/idle). </p><p class="TableIndent">This enables the use of an external inverter on the TX line. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXINV: </span>RX pin active level inversion</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: RX pin signal works using the standard logic levels (V<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">DD</span></sub> = 1/idle, Gnd = 0/mark) </p><p class="TableIndent">1: RX pin signal values are inverted (V<sub><span class="Subscript" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">DD</span></sub> = 0/mark, Gnd = 1/idle). </p><p class="TableIndent">This enables the use of an external inverter on the RX line. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SWAP: </span>Swap TX/RX pins</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: TX/RX pins are used as defined in standard pinout</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The TX and RX pins functions are swapped. This enables to work in the case of a cross-wired connection to another UART. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 13:12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">STOP[1:0]</span>: STOP bits</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are used for programming the stop bits.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: 1 stop bit</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Reserved.</p><p class="TableIndent">10: 2 stop bits</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ADDM7</span>:7-bit address detection/4-bit address detection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is for selection between 4-bit address detection or 7-bit address detection. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: 4-bit address detection</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 7-bit address detection (in 8-bit data mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0)</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>In 7-bit and 9-bit data modes, the address detection is done on 6-bit and 8-bit address (ADD[5:0] and ADD[7:0]) respectively.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 3:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.4 LPUART control register 3 (LPUART\_CR3)

Address offset: 0x08

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

class="TableIndent">001:TXFIFO reaches 1/4 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">110:TXFIFO reaches 1/2 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">011:TXFIFO reaches 3/4 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">100:TXFIFO reaches 7/8 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">101:TXFIFO becomes empty.</p><p class="TableIndent">Remaining combinations: Reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFTIE</span>: RXFIFO threshold interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: An LPUART interrupt is generated when Receive FIFO reaches the threshold programmed in RXFTCFG.</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 27:25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFTCFG[2:0]: </span>Receive FIFO threshold configuration</p><p class="TableIndent">000:Receive FIFO reaches 1/8 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">001:Receive FIFO reaches 1/4 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">110:Receive FIFO reaches 1/2 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">011:Receive FIFO reaches 3/4 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">100:Receive FIFO reaches 7/8 of its depth.</p><p class="TableIndent">101:Receive FIFO becomes full.</p><p class="TableIndent">Remaining combinations: Reserved.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXFTIE</span>: TXFIFO threshold interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A LPUART interrupt is generated when TXFIFO reaches the threshold programmed in TXFTCFG.</p></td></tr><tr height="121.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUFIE</span>: Wake-up from low-power mode interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited</p><p class="TableIndent">1: An LPUART interrupt is generated whenever WUF = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>WUFIE must be set before entering in low-power mode.</p><p class="RegTNote_">If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and must be kept at reset value. Refer to <a href="Universal_synchronous__47_asynchronous_receiver_transmitter_%28USART__47_UART%29.htm#XREF_81814__17_2_General_description24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 40.4: USART implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="166.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 21:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUS[1:0]</span>: Wake-up from low-power mode interrupt flag selection</p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield specifies the event which activates the WUF (wake-up from low-power mode flag). </p><p class="TableIndent">00: WUF active on address match (as defined by ADD[7:0] and ADDM7)</p><p class="TableIndent">01:Reserved.</p><p class="TableIndent">10: WUF active on Start bit detection</p><p class="TableIndent">11: WUF active on RXNE. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bitfield can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and must be kept at reset value. Refer to <a href="Universal_synchronous__47_asynchronous_receiver_transmitter_%28USART__47_UART%29.htm#XREF_81814__17_2_General_description24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 40.4: USART implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DEP</span>: Driver enable polarity selection</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DE signal is active high. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: DE signal is active low.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DEM</span>: Driver enable mode </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables the user to activate the external transceiver control, through the DE signal. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: DE function is disabled. <br/>1: DE function is enabled. The DE signal is output on the RTS pin.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="177.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DDRE</span>: DMA disable on reception error</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA is not disabled in case of reception error. The corresponding error flag is set but RXNE is kept 0 preventing from overrun. As a consequence, the DMA request is not asserted, so the erroneous data is not transferred (no DMA request), but next correct received data is transferred.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: DMA is disabled following a reception error. The corresponding error flag is set, as well as RXNE. The DMA request is masked until the error flag is cleared. This means that the software must first disable the DMA request (DMAR = 0) or clear RXNE before clearing the error flag.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The reception errors are: parity error, framing error or noise error.</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRDIS</span>: Overrun disable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used to disable the receive overrun detection. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Overrun Error Flag, ORE is set when received data is not read before receiving new data. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Overrun functionality is disabled. If new data is received while the RXNE flag is still set</p><p class="TableIndent">the ORE flag is not set and the new received data overwrites the previous content of the LPUART_RDR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This control bit enables checking the communication flow w/o reading the data.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="54.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTSIE</span>: CTS interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited<br/>1: An interrupt is generated whenever CTSIF = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTSE</span>: CTS enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: CTS hardware flow control disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CTS mode enabled, data is only transmitted when the CTS input is deasserted (tied to 0). If the CTS input is asserted while data is being transmitted, then the transmission is completed before stopping. If data is written into the data register while CTS is asserted, the transmission is postponed until CTS is deasserted.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0)</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RTSE</span>: RTS enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: RTS hardware flow control disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: RTS output enabled, data is only requested when there is space in the receive buffer. The transmission of data is expected to cease after the current character has been transmitted. The RTS output is deasserted (pulled to 0) when data can be received.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DMAT</span>: DMA enable transmitter</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by software</p><p class="TableIndent">1: DMA mode is enabled for transmission</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA mode is disabled for transmission</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DMAR</span>: DMA enable receiver</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by software</p><p class="TableIndent">1: DMA mode is enabled for reception</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA mode is disabled for reception</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 5:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">HDSEL</span>: Half-duplex selection</p><p class="TableIndent">Selection of single-wire half-duplex mode </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Half duplex mode is not selected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Half duplex mode is selected </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">EIE</span>: Error interrupt enable</p><p class="TableIndent">Error Interrupt Enable Bit is required to enable interrupt generation in case of a framing error, overrun error or noise flag (FE = 1 or ORE = 1 or NE = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is inhibited<br/>1: An interrupt is generated when FE = 1 or ORE = 1 or NE = 1 in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.5 LPUART baud rate register (LPUART\_BRR)

This register can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0). It may be automatically updated by hardware in auto baud rate detection mode.

Address offset: 0x0C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.90px"/><col width="492.77px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL" style="font-weight:Bold;">BRR[19:0]<span style="font-weight:normal;">: LPUART baud rate</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


**Note:**

It is forbidden to write values lower than 0x300 in the LPUART\_BRR register.

Provided that LPUART\_BRR must be ≧ 0x300 and LPUART\_BRR is 20 bits, a care should be taken when generating high baud rates using high fck values. fck must be in the range [3 x baud rate..4096 x baud rate].

### 41.7.6 LPUART request register (LPUART\_RQR)

Address offset: 0x18

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.54px"/><col width="495.86px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXFRQ</span>: Transmit data flush request</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used when FIFO mode is enabled. TXFRQ bit is set to flush the whole FIFO. This sets the flag TXFE (TXFIFO empty, bit 23 in the LPUART_ISR register). </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>In FIFO mode, the TXFNF flag is reset during the flush request until TxFIFO is empty in order to ensure that no data are written in the data register.</p></td></tr><tr height="56.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFRQ</span>: Receive data flush request</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the RXNE flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This enables discarding the received data without reading it, and avoid an overrun condition.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MMRQ</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Mute mode request</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit puts the LPUART in mute mode and resets the RWU flag.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SBKRQ</span>: Send break request</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit sets the SBKF flag and request to send a BREAK on the line, as soon as the transmit machine is available.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the application needs to send the break character following all previously inserted data, including the ones not yet transmitted, the software should wait for the TXE flag assertion before setting the SBKRQ bit.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.7 LPUART interrupt and status register (LPUART\_ISR)

Address offset: 0x1C

Reset value: 0x0080 00C0

The same register can be used in FIFO mode enabled (this section) and FIFO mode disabled (next section).

FIFO mode enabled

style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">r</span></p></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">r</span></p></td><td style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p class="RegTBodyC"><span class="Arial7" style="background-color:transparent;">r</span></p></td></tr></table></div>


<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.54px"/><col width="499.86px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:28</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 27</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXFT</span>: TXFIFO threshold flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the TXFIFO reaches the threshold programmed in TXFTCFG in LPUART_CR3 register i.e. the TXFIFO contains TXFTCFG empty locations. An interrupt is generated if the TXFTIE bit  = 1 (bit 31) in the LPUART_CR3 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: TXFIFO does not reach the programmed threshold.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: TXFIFO reached the programmed threshold.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 26</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFT</span>: RXFIFO threshold flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the RXFIFO reaches the threshold programmed in RXFTCFG in LPUART_CR3 register i.e. the Receive FIFO contains RXFTCFG data. An interrupt is generated if the RXFTIE bit  = 1 (bit 27) in the LPUART_CR3 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receive FIFO does not reach the programmed threshold.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Receive FIFO reached the programmed threshold.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 25</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 24</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFF</span>: RXFIFO full</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the number of received data corresponds to RXFIFO size + 1 (RXFIFO full + 1 data in the LPUART_RDR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if the RXFFIE bit  = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: RXFIFO is not full</p><p class="TableIndent">1: RXFIFO is full</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXFE</span>: TXFIFO empty</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when TXFIFO is empty. When the TXFIFO contains at least one data, this flag is cleared. The TXFE flag can also be set by writing 1 to the bit TXFRQ (bit 4) in the LPUART_RQR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if the TXFEIE bit  = 1 (bit 30) in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: TXFIFO is not empty</p><p class="TableIndent">1: TXFIFO is empty</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">REACK</span>: Receive enable acknowledge flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware, when the Receive Enable value is taken into account by the LPUART. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can be used to verify that the LPUART is ready for reception before entering low-power mode.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TEACK</span>: Transmit enable acknowledge flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware, when the Transmit Enable value is taken into account by the LPUART. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can be used when an idle frame request is generated by writing TE = 0, followed by TE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register, in order to respect the TE = 0 minimum period. </p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUF</span>: Wake-up from low-power mode flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware, when a wake-up event is detected. The event is defined by the WUS bitfield. It is cleared by software, writing a 1 to the WUCF in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if WUFIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register. </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When UESM is cleared, WUF flag is also cleared.</p><p class="RegTNote_">If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value</p></td></tr><tr height="164.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RWU</span>: Receiver wake-up from mute mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates if the LPUART is in mute mode. It is cleared/set by hardware when a wake-up/mute sequence is recognized. The mute mode control sequence (address or IDLE) is selected by the WAKE bit in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">When wake-up on IDLE mode is selected, this bit can only be set by software, writing 1 to the MMRQ bit in the LPUART_RQR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receiver in Active mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Receiver in mute mode</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SBKF</span>: Send break flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates that a send break character was requested. It is set by software, by writing 1 to the SBKRQ bit in the LPUART_CR3 register. It is automatically reset by hardware during the stop bit of break transmission.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Break character transmitted</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Break character requested by setting SBKRQ bit in LPUART_RQR register</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMF</span>: Character match flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware, when a the character defined by ADD[7:0] is received. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the CMCF in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if CMIE = 1in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Character match detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Character Match detected</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BUSY</span>: Busy flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and reset by hardware. It is active when a communication is ongoing on the RX line (successful start bit detected). It is reset at the end of the reception (successful or not). </p><p class="TableIndent">0: LPUART is idle (no reception)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Reception on going</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTS</span>: CTS flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware. It is an inverted copy of the status of the CTS input pin. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: CTS line set</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CTS line reset</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the hardware flow control feature is not supported, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTSIF</span>: CTS interrupt flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the CTS input toggles, if the CTSE bit is set. It is cleared by software, by writing 1 to the CTSCF bit in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if CTSIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No change occurred on the CTS status line</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A change occurred on the CTS status line</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the hardware flow control feature is not supported, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="194.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXFNF: </span>TXFIFO not full</p><p class="TableIndent">TXFNF is set by hardware when TXFIFO is not full, and so data can be written in the LPUART_TDR. Every write in the LPUART_TDR places the data in the TXFIFO. This flag remains set until the TXFIFO is full. When the TXFIFO is full, this flag is cleared indicating that data can not be written into the LPUART_TDR. </p><p class="TableIndent">The TXFNF is kept reset during the flush request until TXFIFO is empty. After sending the flush request (by setting TXFRQ bit), the flag TXFNF should be checked prior to writing in TXFIFO (TXFNF and TXFE are set at the same time).</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if the TXFNFIE bit  = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Data register is full/Transmit FIFO is full.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Data register/Transmit FIFO is not full.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is used during single buffer transmission.</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TC</span>: Transmission complete</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware if the transmission of a frame containing data is complete and if TXFF is set. An interrupt is generated if TCIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the TCCF in the LPUART_ICR register or by a write to the LPUART_TDR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if TCIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Transmission is not complete</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Transmission is complete</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If TE bit is reset and no transmission is on going, the TC bit is set immediately.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXFNE</span>: RXFIFO not empty</p><p class="TableIndent">RXFNE bit is set by hardware when the RXFIFO is not empty, and so data can be read from the LPUART_RDR register. Every read of the LPUART_RDR frees a location in the RXFIFO. It is cleared when the RXFIFO is empty. </p><p class="TableIndent">The RXFNE flag can also be cleared by writing 1 to the RXFRQ in the LPUART_RQR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if RXFNEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Data is not received</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Received data is ready to be read.</p></td></tr><tr height="165.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IDLE</span>: Idle line detected</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when an Idle line is detected. An interrupt is generated if IDLEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the IDLECF in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Idle line is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Idle line is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The IDLE bit is not set again until the RXFNE bit has been set (i.e. a new idle line occurs).</p><p class="RegTNote_">If mute mode is enabled (MME<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>1), IDLE is set if the LPUART is not mute (RWU<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>0), whatever the mute mode selected by the WAKE bit. If RWU<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>1, IDLE is not set.</p></td></tr><tr height="212.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ORE</span>: Overrun error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the data currently being received in the shift register is</p><p class="TableIndent">ready to be transferred into the LPUART_RDR register while RXFF = 1. It is cleared by a software, writing 1 to the ORECF, in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if RXFNEIE = 1 or EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register, or EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No overrun error</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Overrun error is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When this bit is set, the LPUART_RDR register content is not lost but the shift register is overwritten. An interrupt is generated if the ORE flag is set during multi buffer communication if the EIE bit is set.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This bit is permanently forced to 0 (no overrun detection) when the bit OVRDIS is set in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NE:</span> Start bit noise detection flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when noise is detected on the start bit of a received frame. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the NECF bit in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No noise is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Noise is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit does not generate an interrupt as it appears at the same time as the RXFNE bit which itself generates an interrupt. An interrupt is generated when the NE flag is set during multi buffer communication if the EIE bit is set.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This error is associated with the character in the LPUART_RDR.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FE</span>: Framing error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when a de-synchronization, excessive noise or a break character is detected. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the FECF bit in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">When transmitting data in smartcard mode, this bit is set when the maximum number of transmit attempts is reached without success (the card NACKs the data frame).</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Framing error is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Framing error or break character is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This error is associated with the character in the LPUART_RDR.</p></td></tr><tr height="118.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PE</span>: Parity error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when a parity error occurs in receiver mode. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the PECF in the LPUART_ICR register. <br/>An interrupt is generated if PEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No parity error</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Parity error</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This error is associated with the character in the LPUART_RDR.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.8 LPUART interrupt and status register [alternate] (LPUART\_ISR)

Address offset: 0x1C

Reset value: 0x0000 00C0

The same register can be used in FIFO mode enabled (previous section) and FIFO mode disabled (this section).

FIFO mode disabled

style="border-left-width:0.5pt;border-left-style:solid;border-left-color:#000000;border-bottom-width:0.5pt;border-bottom-style:solid;border-bottom-color:#000000;border-right-width:0.5pt;border-right-style:solid;border-right-color:#000000;border-top-width:0.5pt;border-top-style:solid;border-top-color:#000000;"><p <div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.54px"/><col width="500.86px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:23</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 22</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">REACK</span>: Receive enable acknowledge flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware when the Receive Enable value is taken into account by the LPUART. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can be used to verify that the LPUART is ready for reception before entering low-power mode.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="85.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TEACK</span>: Transmit enable acknowledge flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware, when the Transmit Enable value is taken into account by the LPUART. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can be used when an idle frame request is generated by writing TE = 0, followed by TE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register, in order to respect the TE = 0 minimum period. </p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUF</span>: Wake-up from low-power mode flag </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware, when a wake-up event is detected. The event is defined by the WUS bitfield. It is cleared by software, writing a 1 to the WUCF in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if WUFIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register. </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When UESM is cleared, WUF flag is also cleared.</p><p class="RegTNote_">If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value</p></td></tr><tr height="164.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RWU</span>: Receiver wake-up from mute mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates if the LPUART is in mute mode. It is cleared/set by hardware when a wake-up/mute sequence is recognized. The mute mode control sequence (address or IDLE) is selected by the WAKE bit in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">When wake-up on IDLE mode is selected, this bit can only be set by software, writing 1 to the MMRQ bit in the LPUART_RQR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Receiver in active mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Receiver in mute mode</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SBKF</span>: Send break flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit indicates that a send break character was requested. It is set by software, by writing 1 to the SBKRQ bit in the LPUART_CR3 register. It is automatically reset by hardware during the stop bit of break transmission.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Break character transmitted</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Break character requested by setting SBKRQ bit in LPUART_RQR register</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMF</span>: Character match flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware, when a the character defined by ADD[7:0] is received. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the CMCF in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if CMIE = 1in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Character match detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Character Match detected</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">BUSY</span>: Busy flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and reset by hardware. It is active when a communication is ongoing on the RX line (successful start bit detected). It is reset at the end of the reception (successful or not). </p><p class="TableIndent">0: LPUART is idle (no reception)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Reception on going</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:11</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTS</span>: CTS flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set/reset by hardware. It is an inverted copy of the status of the CTS input pin. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: CTS line set</p><p class="TableIndent">1: CTS line reset</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the hardware flow control feature is not supported, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTSIF</span>: CTS interrupt flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the CTS input toggles, if the CTSE bit is set. It is cleared by software, by writing 1 to the CTSCF bit in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if CTSIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No change occurred on the CTS status line</p><p class="TableIndent">1: A change occurred on the CTS status line</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the hardware flow control feature is not supported, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TXE: </span>Transmit data register empty/TXFIFO not full</p><p class="TableIndent">TXE is set by hardware when the content of the LPUART_TDR register has been transferred into the shift register. It is cleared by a write to the LPUART_TDR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if the TXEIE bit =1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Data register full</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Data register not full</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is used during single buffer transmission.</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TC</span>: Transmission complete</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware if the transmission of a frame containing data is complete and if TXE is set. An interrupt is generated if TCIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the TCCF in the LPUART_ICR register or by a write to the LPUART_TDR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if TCIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Transmission is not complete</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Transmission is complete</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If TE bit is reset and no transmission is on going, the TC bit is immediately set.</p></td></tr><tr height="132.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RXNE</span>: Read data register not empty</p><p class="TableIndent">RXNE bit is set by hardware when the content of the LPUART_RDR shift register has been transferred to the LPUART_RDR register. It is cleared by reading from the LPUART_RDR register. The RXNE flag can also be cleared by writing 1 to the RXFRQ in the LPUART_RQR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if RXNEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Data is not received</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Received data is ready to be read.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IDLE</span>: Idle line detected</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when an Idle Line is detected. An interrupt is generated if IDLEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the IDLECF in the LPUART_ICR register. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Idle line is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Idle line is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>The IDLE bit is not set again until the RXNE bit has been set (i.e. a new idle line occurs).</p><p class="RegTNote_">If mute mode is enabled (MME<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>1), IDLE is set if the LPUART is not mute (RWU<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>0), whatever the mute mode selected by the WAKE bit. If RWU<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>1, IDLE is not set.</p></td></tr><tr height="228.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ORE</span>: Overrun error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when the data currently being received in the shift register is</p><p class="TableIndent">ready to be transferred into the LPUART_RDR register while RXNE = 1. It is cleared by a software, writing 1 to the ORECF, in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if RXNEIE = 1 or EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register, or EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent"><br/></p><p class="TableIndent">0: No overrun error</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Overrun error is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When this bit is set, the LPUART_RDR register content is not lost but the shift register is overwritten. An interrupt is generated if the ORE flag is set during multi buffer communication if the EIE bit is set.</p><p class="RegTNote_">This bit is permanently forced to 0 (no overrun detection) when the bit OVRDIS is set in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NE:</span> Start bit noise detection flag</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when noise is detected on the start bit of a received frame. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the NECF bit in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No noise is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Noise is detected</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit does not generate an interrupt as it appears at the same time as the RXNE bit which itself generates an interrupt. An interrupt is generated when the NE flag is set during multi buffer communication if the EIE bit is set.</p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FE</span>: Framing error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when a de-synchronization, excessive noise or a break character is detected. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the FECF bit in the LPUART_ICR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">When transmitting data in smartcard mode, this bit is set when the maximum number of transmit attempts is reached without success (the card NACKs the data frame).</p><p class="TableIndent">An interrupt is generated if EIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR3 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No Framing error is detected</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Framing error or break character is detected</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PE</span>: Parity error</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by hardware when a parity error occurs in receiver mode. It is cleared by software, writing 1 to the PECF in the LPUART_ICR register. <br/>An interrupt is generated if PEIE = 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No parity error</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Parity error</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.9 LPUART interrupt flag clear register (LPUART\_ICR)

Address offset: 0x20

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.54px"/><col width="495.86px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:21</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 20</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WUCF</span>: Wake-up from low-power mode clear flag </p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the WUF flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>If the LPUART does not support the wake-up from Stop feature, this bit is reserved and kept at reset value. Refer to <a href="Universal_synchronous__47_asynchronous_receiver_transmitter_%28USART__47_UART%29.htm#XREF_81814__17_2_General_description24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 40.4: USART implementation</a>.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 19:18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMCF</span>: Character match clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the CMF flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 16:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CTSCF</span>: CTS clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the CTSIF flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 8:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TCCF</span>: Transmission complete clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the TC flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">IDLECF</span>: Idle line detected clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the IDLE flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">ORECF</span>: Overrun error clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the ORE flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NECF</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Noise detected clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the NE flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FECF</span>: Framing error clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the FE flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="40.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PECF</span>: Parity error clear flag</p><p class="TableIndent">Writing 1 to this bit clears the PE flag in the LPUART_ISR register.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.10 LPUART receive data register (LPUART\_RDR)

Address offset: 0x24

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.81px"/><col width="494.04px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 8:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">RDR[8:0]</span>: Receive data value</p><p class="TableIndent">Contains the received data character.</p><p class="TableIndent">The RDR register provides the parallel interface between the input shift register and the internal bus (see <a href="#XREF_23415__Figure_267__LPUART_block_diagram24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Figure 591</a>).</p><p class="TableIndent">When receiving with the parity enabled, the value read in the MSB bit is the received parity bit.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.11 transmit data register (LPUART LPUART\_TDR)

Address offset: 0x28

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.81px"/><col width="494.04px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="133.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 8:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TDR[8:0]</span>: Transmit data value</p><p class="TableIndent">Contains the data character to be transmitted.</p><p class="TableIndent">The TDR register provides the parallel interface between the internal bus and the output shift register (see <a href="#XREF_23415__Figure_267__LPUART_block_diagram24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Figure 591</a>).</p><p class="TableIndent">When transmitting with the parity enabled (PCE bit set to 1 in the LPUART_CR1 register), the value written in the MSB (bit 7 or bit 8 depending on the data length) has no effect because it is replaced by the parity.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This register must be written only when TXE/TXFNF<span style="font-style:normal;"> = </span>1.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 41.7.12 LPUART prescaler register (LPUART\_PRESC)

This register can only be written when the LPUART is disabled (UE = 0).

Address offset: 0x2C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left" data-conditional="_sci3_baudgen_type"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="115.81px"/><col width="494.04px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="313.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 3:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PRESCALER[3:0]</span>: Clock prescaler</p><p class="TableBodyL">The LPUART input clock can be divided by a prescaler:</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: input clock not divided</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: input clock divided by 2</p><p class="TableIndent">0010: input clock divided by 4</p><p class="TableIndent">0011: input clock divided by 6</p><p class="TableIndent">0100: input clock divided by 8</p><p class="TableIndent">0101: input clock divided by 10</p><p class="TableIndent">0110: input clock divided by 12</p><p class="TableIndent">0111: input clock divided by 16</p><p class="TableIndent">1000: input clock divided by 32</p><p class="TableIndent">1001: input clock divided by 64</p><p class="TableIndent">1010: input clock divided by 128</p><p class="TableIndent">1011: input clock divided by 256</p><p class="TableIndent">Remaining combinations: Reserved.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When PRESCALER is programmed with a value different of the allowed ones, programmed prescaler value is 1011 i.e. input clock divided by 256.</p><p class="RegTNote_">If the prescaler is not supported, this bitfield is reserved and must be kept at reset value. Refer to <a href="#XREF_89270__52_3_LPUART_implementation24" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 41.3: LPUART implementation on page 1777</a>.</p></td></tr></table></div>
